2026-05-31 17:02:19 Brennan -> AI OFM FRAMEWORK:
Body proportions are a bit weird. Especially with the full length shots. That happens sometimes where the head and body proportions don't match. Usually you can just change prompt or regenerate and it is solved. The horse photos are much more realistic and believable IMO.

AI works from sampling from a statistical norm. The more you pull away from that statistical norm the more issues and the more random and incoherent your generations will be. AI will try to snap to the highest probable "solution" to what it has seen from its training data.

The statistically common "regions" are "well represented" so it can generate reliably. When you do something weird it has never seen (16 year old innocent face, huge boobs, BBL and body of a 30 year old model) it gets confused. It doesn't know what to do, so it "guesses" more. It doesn't have enough examples to handle that scenario reliably so the output quality is more random and you maybe get weird stuff.

2026-06-02 18:33:51 Brennan -> AI OFM FRAMEWORK:
EXIF remove. It's cheap and easy. Removing SynthId is only lowering the probability. The more you want it to not be detected the more you need to degrade your image / video. You also need a GPU that can run diffusion passes to recreate the image / video. That can be expensive to setup and run. So up to you. And also, not a lot of things are checking SynthId automated. Much more common is using watermark classifiers or AI classifiers, not SynthId specifically. SynthId is much more specific than that.

Personally I think stripping EXIF and not worrying about the stuff the would require you to degrade your image quality for the time being.

2026-06-03 16:10:53 Brennan -> AI OFM FRAMEWORK:
Also you guys are confusing SIM and location.
SIM is about identity.
Phone numbers associate to a SIM identity.
Phones can have 1 or more SIM identities.
SIM identities can have 1 or more phone numbers associated with them.

The location is where it looks like you are connected from (Geo IP lookup). That has absolutely nothing to do with whether you are using a US number or whether your SIM is US.

Type of the IP is also a factor:

Known VPN: bad signal
Datacenter IP: bad signal
Residential home IP: good signal
Mobile IP: neutral to slightly positive signal

2026-06-03 16:30:12 Brennan -> AI OFM FRAMEWORK:
Instagram can see several things:
1. Your phone number that you verify with (conveys country)
2. Your IP address (conveys location, type of IP, known IP of bad actors)
3. Agreement between your phone number and the location IP (do they match the same location / country or do they disagree)

These are all factors.

Best is a USA phone number and a residential IP or mobile IP that is also in the USA.

Keep the image slightly imperfect and naturally compressed like a real uploaded Instagram or Snapchat photo. Avoid the polished AI beauty look. Make it indistinguishable from a real human iPhone photo. 

NEGATIVE PROMPT:

AI look, CGI, plastic skin, beauty filter, airbrushed skin, overprocessed face, hyper sharp details, doll face, studio lighting, cinematic lighting, fashion photoshoot, unreal symmetry, fake eyes, fake teeth, fake hair, perfect makeup, smooth skin, wax skin, generated texture, unrealistic anatomy, distorted hands, extra fingers, duplicate limbs, warped background, oversaturated colors, fake shadows, 3D render, ultra clean image, unreal clarity, artificial sharpness, glamour render, fake HDR, polished AI face

2026-08-02 02:55:19 Brennan -> AI OFM FRAMEWORK:
More photos = less training per photo so it's harder to lock things in. But too few photos and it will over memorize things you don't want like a particular expression, angle, ligthing style, etc, so there's a sweet spot. Training is a a lot of trial and error. What works for one person might work slightly different for another. There's thousands of variables.

Also, don't have multiple photos that look almost the same. You want variety. Vary it up. Don't do all headshots, don't do multiple of the same background, expression, lighting. Vary things up as much as possible while still keeping your model looking the same.

2026-08-04 23:38:15 Brennan -> AI OFM FRAMEWORK:
Metadata is different from an AI detection image classifier. As is SynthId. Those are 3 different things. Most just worry about removing metadata and that's it. To remove SynthId and/or lower the AI detection score you need to degrade your image which is not desirable from a quality perspective. Most people running models at scale don't worry about AI classifiers, only the metadata.
